            x: OptPairTensor = (x, x)        
        x_in = x[0]
        
        # propagate_type: (x: OptPairTensor, edge_attr: OptTensor)
        if edge_attr is not None:
          sndr_node_attr = torch.gather(x_in, 0, edge_index[0:1,:].repeat(x_in.shape[1], 1).T)
          rcvr_node_attr = torch.gather(x_in, 0, edge_index[1:2,:].repeat(x_in.shape[1], 1).T)
          
          edge_attr = edge_attr + (sndr_node_attr - rcvr_node_attr).abs()
          
        latent = self.propagate(edge_index=edge_index, x=x, edge_attr=edge_attr, size=size) 
        
        """ Added a linear layer to manage dimensionality """
        if mlp:

        else:

        if residual:
            latent = latent + x[1]
        
        #del x, edge_index, edge_attr 
        if mlp:
            latent = self.mlp(latent)  
        return latent       

    def message(self, x_j: Tensor, edge_attr: OptTensor) -> Tensor:
        """ Concatenating edge features instead of adding those to node features """
        
        msg = x_j if edge_attr is None else torch.cat((x_j, edge_attr), dim=1)
        #print(f'msg.shape = {msg.shape}')
        #del x_j, edge_attr
        return F.selu(msg) + self.eps

    def __repr__(self) -> str:

class m_GCN(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self,name=None, in_dim=1, out_dim=1, edge_dim=0, latent_dim=32, n_aggr=45, n_hops=1, bias=False, num_layers=2, dropout=0., batch_size=32, w_sigmoid=True):
        super(m_GCN, self).__init__()
        self.name = f'mGCN-n_aggr{n_aggr}-nhops{n_hops}-nmlp{num_layers}' if name is None else name
        self.n_aggr = n_aggr
        self.n_hops = n_hops
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self.latent = latent_dim
        self.out_dim = out_dim
        self.w_sigmoid = w_sigmoid
        self.node_in = torch.nn.Linear(in_dim, latent_dim, bias=bias)        
        self.node_out = torch.nn.Linear(latent_dim, out_dim, bias=bias)
        self.edge = torch.nn.Linear(edge_dim, latent_dim, bias=bias)        
            
        self.gcn_aggrs = torch.nn.ModuleList()
        for _ in range(n_aggr):
            gcn = GENConvolution(latent_dim, latent_dim, latent_dim, aggr="add", bias=bias, num_layers=num_layers, dropout=dropout)
            self.gcn_aggrs.append(gcn)

        
    def forward(self, x, edge_index, batch = None, edge_attr=None):
        #print(f'edge_attr type = {type(edge_attr)}')
        
        #print(f'edge_index shape = {edge_index.shape}')
        #print(f'sndr_node_attr shape = {sndr_node_attr.shape}')
        #print(f'rcvr_node_attr shape = {rcvr_node_attr.shape}')
        #print(f'x_in shape = {x_in.shape}')
        #print(f'edge_attr shape = {edge_attr.shape}')
        """ Embedding for edge features. """
        if edge_attr is not None:
            edge_attr = self.edge(edge_attr)
        """ Embedding for node features. """
        Z = self.node_in(x)

        """ 
            Mutiple GCN layers.
        """
        for i,gcn in enumerate(self.gcn_aggrs):
            """
                Multiple Hops.
            """
            #print(f'checking gcn_{i}')
            for _ in range(self.n_hops - 1):
                Z = torch.selu(gcn(x=Z, edge_index=edge_index, edge_attr=edge_attr,  mlp=False))
            Z = torch.selu(gcn(x=Z, edge_index=edge_index, edge_attr=edge_attr,  mlp=True))
                
        """ Reconstructing node features through a final dense layer. """
        y_predict = self.node_out(Z)
        if self.w_sigmoid:
            y_predict = F.sigmoid(y_predict)
        return y_predict

Their difference is used to diagnose leaks in official SCADA, including the 2019 evaluation series. name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

def tex(s):
 return ''.join({'\\':r'\textbackslash{}','&':r'\&','%':r'\%','$':r'\$','#':r'\#','_':r'\_','{':r'\{','}':r'\}','~':r'\textasciitilde{}','^':r'\textasciicircum{}'}.get(c,c) for c in s)
class HTMLTex(HTMLParser):
 def __init__(self):super().__init__();self.parts=[]
 def handle_starttag(self,tag,attrs):
  attr=dict(attrs)
  if tag=='b':self.parts.append(r'\textbf{')
  elif tag=='font':self.parts.append(r'\texttt{')
  elif tag=='link':self.parts.append(r'\href{'+attr['href'].replace('%',r'\%').replace('#',r'\#')+'}{')
  elif tag=='br':self.parts.append(r'\par ')
 def handle_endtag(self,tag):
  if tag in ['b','font','link']:self.parts.append('}')
 def handle_data(self,s):self.parts.append(tex(s))
def html(s):p=HTMLTex();p.feed(s);return ''.join(p.parts)
def table(rows,widths):
 n=len(widths);ratios=[w/sum(widths)*n for w in widths]
 cols=''.join(r'>{\hsize='+f'{r:.5f}'+r'\hsize\raggedright\arraybackslash}X' for r in ratios)
 return '\n'.join([r'{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.16}',r'\rowcolors{2}{PaleBlue}{white}',r'\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}'+cols+r'@{}}',r'\rowcolor{Blue!13} '+' & '.join(r'\textbf{'+html(c)+'}' for c in rows[0])+r' \\',*[' & '.join((r'\textbf{\color{Navy}'+html(c)+'}' if i==0 else html(c)) for i,c in enumerate(row))+r' \\' for row in rows[1:]],r'\end{tabularx}\par}',r'\vspace{1.7mm}'])

section=data.get('section_titles',['Dataset and predictive task','Previous learning solutions','Implementation and course connections','Contribution and evaluation','Discussion and references'])
section_numbers=data.get('section_numbers',list(range(1,page_count+1)))
assert len(section_numbers)==page_count
pdf_title=data.get('pdf_title','DS675 Mini-Project Milestone 2 - Working draft v'+version)
header_label=data.get('header_label','Draft v'+version)
parts=[r'\input{milestone_visual.tex}',r'\usepackage{graphicx}',r'\hypersetup{pdftitle={'+tex(pdf_title)+r'}}',r'\begin{document}\small']
for index,page in enumerate(data['pages']):
 if index:parts.append(r'\newpage')
 page_label=(tex(header_label)+' | ' if header_label else '')+str(index+1)+'/'+str(page_count)
 parts.append(r'\milestoneheader{2}{Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting}{'+page_label+'}')
 continuation=' (continued)' if index and section_numbers[index]==section_numbers[index-1] else ''
 parts.append(r'\sectiontitle{'+str(section_numbers[index])+'. '+section[index]+continuation+'}')
 for block in page:
  kind=block['kind'];args=block['args']
  if kind=='title':continue
  if kind=='p' and args[0].startswith('<b>DS675 Mini-Project'):continue
  if kind=='h':
   # Section name is now in the shared Navy section title.
   if re.match(r'^\d+\.\s',args[0]):continue
   parts.append(r'\vspace{1.5mm}\labeltext{'+html(args[0])+'}'+r'\par\vspace{.7mm}')
  elif kind=='table':parts.append(table(*args))
  elif kind=='image':
   shutil.copyfile(pub/args[0],build/args[0])
   parts.append(r'\begin{center}\includegraphics[width=\linewidth,height='+str(args[2])+r'in,keepaspectratio]{'+args[0]+r'}\end{center}{\footnotesize '+html(args[1])+r'\par}\vspace{1.8mm}')
  elif kind=='code':parts.append(r'\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=PaleBlue,colframe=Teal,boxrule=.4pt,arc=1mm,left=2mm,right=2mm,top=1mm,bottom=1mm]\footnotesize\begin{verbatim}'+'\n'+args[0]+'\n'+r'\end{verbatim}'+'\n'+r'\end{tcolorbox}')
  elif kind=='math':parts.append(r'\['+args[0]+r'\]')
  elif kind=='p':
   text=html(args[0])
   if args[0].startswith('<b>Draft status.'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{notebox}'+text+r'\end{notebox}\vspace{1mm}')
   elif section[index].startswith('Search') and args[0].startswith('GarÃ°arsson et al. report'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{contextbox}'+text+r'\end{contextbox}')
   elif section[index].startswith('Prior work') and args[0].startswith('We propose to investigate'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{datasetbox}[Teal]{Candidate contribution}'+text+r'\end{datasetbox}\vspace{1mm}')
   elif index==page_count-1 and args[0].startswith('Before submission'):
    parts.append(r'\begin{notebox}'+text+r'\end{notebox}\vspace{1mm}')
   else:parts.append(text+r'\par\vspace{1.7mm}')
 if index==page_count-1:
  parts.append(r'\vspace{1mm}{\footnotesize')
  for label,url in data['references']:parts.append(r'\href{'+url+'}{'+tex(label)+r'}\par\vspace{1.4mm}')
  parts.append('}')
parts.append(r'\end{document}')
shutil.copyfile(style,build/'milestone_visual.tex')
source=build/'Milestone2-borrador.tex';source.write_text('\n'.join(parts),encoding='utf-8')
for _ in range(2):
 run=subprocess.run(['pdflatex','-interaction=nonstopmode','-halt-on-error',source.name],cwd=build,capture_output=True,text=True,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace',timeout=120)
 if run.returncode:
  print(run.stdout[-6500:]);raise SystemExit(run.returncode)
pdf=build/'Milestone2-borrador.pdf'
with pymupdf.open(pdf) as doc:

local=project/('Milestone 2/entregables/borrador-v'+version);local.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
for p in [pdf,source,style]:shutil.copyfile(p,local/p.name)
for page in data['pages']:
 for block in page:
  if block['kind']=='image':shutil.copyfile(build/block['args'][0],local/block['args'][0])

master=project/'Plantilla';master.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True);shutil.copyfile(style,master/style.name)

for row in provenance['outputs']:

if not options.archive_only:(web/'docs/MILESTONE_PREVIEWS.json').write_text(json.dumps(provenance,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
print(json.dumps({'pages':page_count,'version':version,'sha256':hashlib.sha256(pdf.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'overfull_hboxes':log.count('Overfull \\hbox'),'previous_draft_preserved':str(project/'Milestone 2/entregables/borrador-v0.2/Milestone2-borrador.pdf')}))

def check(name):
    rel='miniproject/milestone-2/'+name
    with urllib.request.urlopen(base+rel+'?rev='+token,timeout=30) as r:data=r.read()
    assert data==(W/'dist'/rel).read_bytes(),rel
    return {'file':rel,'bytes':len(data),'sha256':hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest(),'matches_local':True}

      {
        "kind": "h",
        "args": [
          "3.2 ChebNet: forecasting and reconstruction as complementary diagnostic models"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Task and preparation.</b> Gardarsson, Boem and Toni [3] train a predictor of next-step nodal pressure and a reconstructor of the current pressure field. Both learn from pressure generated by the nominal hydraulic model; sparse pressure observations and topology are inputs, while full simulated nodal pressures supply labels. Their difference is used to diagnose leaks in official SCADA, including the 2019 evaluation series. Graph-weight/scaling choices, observed-node handling and historical windows are explored; residual plots examine how leakage changes the learned relationships."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Model and time alignment.</b> Four Chebyshev graph-convolution layers propagate pressure features through the pipe graph. Hidden polynomial orders [240,120,20] and filter sizes [120,60,30], SiLU hidden activations, sigmoid output and Adam/weight decay define the inspected architecture [3,6]. History is stacked as input channels. T=3 is selected from {1,2,3,6,12,24}; at five-minute sampling it is a 15-minute history. The pinned loader ends X at row i-1 and labels row i: the forecast lead is +5 minutes, not +15."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Performance and significance.</b> Predictor-minus-reconstructor residuals are mapped from nodes to edges and analyzed with seven-day moving statistics, alpha=1 and a one-alarm-per-day/six-hop neighborhood filter. The article reports 19 TP, 138 FP, 4 FN, TPR=82.61% and EUR326,521 for leakage diagnosis (Table 1, pp.5-6). A match requires an alarm during the leak lifespan and within 300 m. This demonstrates useful sparse graph inference, but also a substantial false-alarm burden; it is not a reported pressure MAE for n1(+15)."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Code and transfer to our study.</b> <link href=\"https://github.com/GardarGardarsson/GSP_for_Leak_Detection/blob/864a99041af6331375946a8fad3a71c633108eb8/04%20Leakage%20Detection/utils/data_loader.py#L285-L290\">Window/label construction</link> and <link href=\"https://github.com/GardarGardarsson/GSP_for_Leak_Detection/blob/864a99041af6331375946a8fad3a71c633108eb8/04%20Leakage%20Detection/predict.py#L120-L131\">preparation and chronological 80/20 split</link> are directly inspectable [6]. Scaling precedes that split in the selected script; our protocol fits transforms on training only. A nominal healthy reference for diagnosis and a forecast intended to follow recorded pressure during a leak have different target meanings."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "h",
        "args": [
          "3.3 SAM-kNN: adaptive local virtual pressure sensors"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Task, data and preparation.</b> Jakob et al. [4] simulate ten three-month WNTR scenarios on modified L-Town Area A (661 nodes, 766 links, 29 pressure sensors), sampled every five minutes. Five scenarios have one leak each; five have a sensor fault. These are newly generated scenarios, not the original 2019 SCADA mixture. The selected <link href=\"https://github.com/andreArtelt/SAM-kNN-Regressor_OnlineLearning_WDNs/blob/9116ec99bf9f053e35401fe3e388062e8b979c54/Implementation/LeakDbScenarioPreprocessing.py#L19-L27\">preprocessor</link> averages three preceding rows and labels the fourth. For each target sensor, its own input channel is excluded: the virtual sensor forecasts the next reading from the other sensors."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Model and evaluation.</b> Short-term, long-term and combined memories generate competing kNN predictions; tracked errors select a memory, with cleaning/compression to retain useful older concepts while adapting. The pinned experiment skips the first 100 raw samples, initializes from 3,000 processed rows and then predicts batches of 200 before updating. Comparators are online kNN (k=5, window=500) and linear regression. Pressure discrepancies trigger alarms; the published leak results are:"

          "<b>Interpretation and code.</b> Table 1 (p.10) counts whether the single event was detected for TP/FN, but counts every false alarm for FP. Thus the large FP reduction is an anomaly-detection result, not pressure MAE or row-wise classification accuracy. <link href=\"https://github.com/andreArtelt/SAM-kNN-Regressor_OnlineLearning_WDNs/blob/9116ec99bf9f053e35401fe3e388062e8b979c54/Implementation/SAM_KNN_Regression.py\">Adaptive memories</link> and <link href=\"https://github.com/andreArtelt/SAM-kNN-Regressor_OnlineLearning_WDNs/blob/9116ec99bf9f053e35401fe3e388062e8b979c54/Implementation/experiments.py\">stream ordering/configuration</link> support the mechanism [7]; scenario data are not supplied in the repository.     [
      {
        "kind": "h",
        "args": [
          "3.4 AGN: multistep future heads with graph attention and nodal attributes"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Task and interpretation.</b> Zou et al. [11] describe demand-state forecasting \"from a pressure perspective.\" The method forecasts future nodal heads (Eq.10; Fig.3), with pressure histories, node heights and node types as inputs. At a fixed node elevation, pressure head is hydraulic head minus elevation. This makes it a genuine temporal pressure-related antecedent, while preserving the paper's distinction between measured pressure inputs and future-head outputs."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Data exploration and preparation.</b> The authors plot elevation, node-use types and annual consumption patterns (Fig.2). They randomly assign 104 residential/commercial/industrial demand patterns to the 782 demand nodes and generate one year at five-minute resolution. An added pumping-station node yields a 783-node evaluation. These modified-network sequences are split chronologically 70/20/10; they are not the original recorded 2018/2019 SCADA outcomes. Pump/PRV connections receive special adjacency weights (300 and 1, respectively), while other edges use a hydraulic-proximity weighting derived from pipe properties (Secs.2.1,3.1)."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Learning model.</b> AGN starts from a gated graph/GRU formulation. Weighted neighbor propagation exchanges local information; self-attention connects distant node histories; external attention combines pressure states with elevation/use attributes; gated hidden states and a cascade output layer generate the future sequence. Training loss uses sensor-node targets; non-sensor outputs are generalized using nodal attributes. The authors compare AGN with GGNN and graph-head reconstruction followed by GRU (GHR+GRU). All have hidden dimension 12, Adam and batch size 32; AGN/GGNN use three recurrent layers, with an initial learning rate of 0.001 and epoch decay factor 0.8 (Sec.3.2)."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Forecast construction and measured performance.</b> History length equals forecast-window length: 12,24,48 or 96 five-minute steps. The windows end at +60,+120,+240 or +480 minutes and include all intervening steps. In particular, the third output is +15 minutes; selecting that horizon alone cannot establish novelty. Table 1 reports MAE and MAPE aggregated over forecast steps and nodes within elevation regions:"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "table",
        "args": [
          [

      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Significance and limits.</b> The method combines temporal forecasting with sparse-to-global inference and provides feature-masking and layer-depth analyses. Its regional results also expose a serious low-elevation failure: good accuracy is not uniform across the network. Longer windows change both the information supplied and the forecast target set, so Table 1 is not a controlled curve of error versus lead with fixed history. It does not report the isolated n1(+15) metric needed for our experiment."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Code and reproducibility.</b> The <link href=\"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S259012302502969X\">primary article</link> supplies equations, settings and regional results; its data statement says data are available on request. No repository hyperlink was found among its rendered article links in this inspection; despite an \"open-source\" wording in the conclusion, we cannot provide an inspected implementation link. This is a documented access limit, not a claim that code does not exist. The authors also acknowledge possible leakage during model tuning; our evaluation freezes choices before the final assessment."
        ]
      }

    [
      {
        "kind": "h",
        "args": [
          "3.5 LILA: a regression reference followed by change detection and hydraulic localization"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Task, analysis and preparation.</b> Daniel et al. [8] seek leak events and locations rather than future pressure. They examine correlated pressure behavior, select reference periods and sensor combinations, and fit relations expected within the same hydraulic condition. The publication derives pressure/flow reference models with squared-flow terms; the pinned <link href=\"https://github.com/SWN-group-at-TU-Berlin/LILA/blob/b488a60db1974b6f5b7887ce1cf5d31dff3dad68/_utils/BattLeDIM_utils.py#L204-L251\">implementation</link> fits LinearRegression using another sensor's current pressure and raw PUMP_1 flow. This version-specific difference must be preserved, not silently equated."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Model.</b> Pressure-reference residuals form a most-affected-sensor trajectory. CUSUM accumulates changes, using variability-related thresholds, to identify a change point; calibrated hydraulic pressure-drop signatures and optimization identify candidate pipes. Re-estimation across states helps handle overlapping leaks. Manually selected reference windows and ex-post event annotations support the published analysis, so a prospective reproduction must verify what would have been known at each time."
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Results.</b> The journal evaluation includes 19 leaks starting in 2019, excluding four inherited from 2018. Table 1 uses a 300 m localization tolerance and detection allowed during the leak lifespan:"
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "table",
        "args": [
          [
            [
              "Method in the journal comparison",
              "TP / FP / FN",
              "Recall / precision / F1",
              "Economic score"
            ],
            [
              "LILA",
              "17 / 2 / 0",
              "1.000 / 0.895 / 0.944",
              "EUR307,852"
            ],
            [
              "Paper's BattLeDIM benchmark",
              "10 / 8 / 1",
              "0.909 / 0.556 / 0.690",
              "EUR191,055"
            ]
          ],
          [
            168,
            96,
            150,
            90
          ]
        ]
      },
      {
        "kind": "p",
        "args": [
          "<b>Significance and boundary.</b> All 19 events were identified, but two localizations fell outside 300 m and count as FP. Recall=1 therefore does not mean every location was accepted.           "<b>Task and preparation.</b> Truong et al. [10] reconstruct current pressures at unobserved junctions from sparse same-time pressures. Hydraulic simulations vary operating parameters; random sensor masks expose the model to changing observation layouts. Masked inputs are zero-filled and loss is computed at masked positions. For L-Town, 10,000 generated snapshots are split 60/20/20. A separate one-week demand-pattern series sampled every five minutes provides the temporal-pattern test (Sec.6.4). Sequential testing still processes each snapshot independently: there is no future-pressure target."

Density plots compare generated-snapshot and demand-pattern distributions, demonstrating why testing on replicas of the training distribution can be optimistic. These preparation and evaluation choices are important contributions beyond the attention architecture."
